## Scoring Formula

```text
priority_score =
    impact + alignment + learning + confidence
  − effort − risk
  + release_blocker_bonus      # +3 if item is in SYSTEM_HEALTH.md Release Blockers
  − saturation_penalty          # −2 if 3 of last 5 iterations landed in the same Area
```

Scoring scale:
- 1 = very low
- 3 = medium
- 5 = very high

Higher total score = higher priority. Post Meta-Review 001: range widened to ~6–18 (was 10–16).

### Saturation status (computed over iter 011–016 — rolling 5 bounded loops; iter 015 is Mode 4 and does not consume Area cadence)

- Extension-architecture / segmentation area = 1 of last 5 (iter 011)
- Invariants / testing area = 2 of last 5 (iter 012, iter 013) — cluster cleared; 2 more non-invariants loops aged it out
- UX resilience area = 1 of last 5 (iter 014)
- Web-app UI area = 1 of last 5 (iter 016) — **new entry**; first web-app bounded loop since iter 001, partial Signal-5 portfolio-drift relief
- Session-durability area = 0 of last 5 (iter 010 aged out)
- No 3-in-a-row; no `−S` penalties currently apply. Area diversity remains strong (4 distinct areas in rolling 5-loop window).

### Portfolio override rules

See `CLAUDE.md § Selection Policy` — any of these overrides top-score:
1. Release-blocker minimum cadence (1-in-5)
2. Area saturation rule
3. Follow-up burn-down (1-in-5)

| ~~92~~ | ~~**PIB-P06** ErrorBoundary on dashboard surface~~ — **CLOSED loop 56.** Two placements, deliberately different in scope: one around the shell (wrapping it from OUTSIDE its own render, since a boundary cannot catch what it throws itself) and **one around each row**, which is the placement that turns a blank page into a list with one gap in it. Before this, a single malformed workflow took the whole route to the 500 page — forty working workflows made invisible by one. **The reporting decision is the interesting part:** `componentDidCatch` emits `ui_error_boundary_triggered` with the surface and the error's CONSTRUCTOR NAME only, never the message or stack. Messages here interpolate what broke, and what broke is a workflow title, a step label or a field name captured from a user's screen — a crash report is the obvious path for recorded content to leak into analytics. Full detail goes to the server log instead. Enforced three ways: a pure `describeBoundaryError` that rejects a `name` containing whitespace or over 40 chars (a message wearing a name), a `@ts-expect-error` proving a `message` field cannot attach to the event, and a source assertion that `.message` appears nowhere in the module. The row's Sentry suggestion was not taken — no Sentry is installed, and inventing a dependency was not in scope. Original text: **PIB-P06** ErrorBoundary on dashboard surface — current `DashboardV2Shell` has no React ErrorBoundary; an uncaught exception in any descendant (`WorkflowList`, `WorkflowRow`, `HealthTooltip`, `KebabMenu`) crashes the entire dashboard route to the Next.js 500 page. World-class platforms degrade gracefully — partial-failure UI showing what loaded vs what failed. Frontend F1 finding. Fix: wrap `DashboardV2Shell` content in `ErrorBoundary` component with degraded state UI ("Some workflows failed to render — refresh to retry") + Sentry/PostHog error capture; isolate per-row errors via inner ErrorBoundary on `WorkflowRow` so single-row failure doesn't blanket-crash the list. ~80 LOC + 6-8 tests. | 94 | **RE-SCOPED at loop 60 — the stated harm was wrong, the real one is larger, and the prescribed fix would have lost events.** **(a) Not a contamination.** The row says events emit `userPlan: undefined`, contaminating plan-tier analysis. `analytics.ts:851` read `if (userPlan != null) base.userPlan = userPlan` — the property was **omitted entirely**, so those events were silently EXCLUDED from any plan breakdown rather than mis-attributed. Quieter and worse: an analysis that drops an unknown share of its input looks complete. **(b) Not a race — a permanent absence almost everywhere.** `setUserPlanForAnalytics` has **exactly one caller**, inside the dashboard's own fetch (`DashboardV2Shell.tsx:372`), so every event on every other authenticated page — SOP view, upload, settings — carried no plan at all, as did dashboard events fired before that fetch resolved. **(c) The prescribed fix would have lost data.** Queue-until-plan-set then drain: `dashboard_bounced` fires from `beforeunload` via `sendBeacon`, so anything still queued at that moment is never sent — trading a visible gap for missing events. **DONE at loop 60:** the plan is now always present, emitting the literal `'unknown'` when unresolved, so the gap appears as a bucket in the breakdown instead of vanishing from it. **STILL OPEN — the substantive half:** make the plan known globally rather than only on the dashboard. Two paths, neither free: add `plan` to the NextAuth JWT/session (no extra fetch; goes stale after an upgrade until the token refreshes — acceptable for segmentation, but it touches the login path for every user), or fetch `/api/account` once in `AppShell` (fresh, but that endpoint is **already** fetched twice per dashboard load per row #189, so this should be done together with that de-duplication rather than adding a third). Original text: **PIB-P08** userPlan analytics race-condition fix (gate emission on plan-set) — iter 038 introduced `setUserPlanForAnalytics` side-channel writing `window.__ledgerium_userPlan` consumed by `track()` IS_BROWSER enrichment; analytics F2 finding identified that `track()` calls firing BEFORE the userPlan side-channel is set (e.g., on initial component mount before `fetchWorkflows` resolves) emit events with `userPlan: undefined`, contaminating the bounce-rate-by-plan-tier analysis. Fix: gate `track()` emission on userPlan-set (queue events fired before plan-set, drain on plan-set; OR delay emission until plan-set resolves with bounded timeout). ~40 LOC + 6-8 tests covering pre-set / post-set / timeout / queue-drain semantics. `analytics` PRIMARY + `frontend-engineer` adjacent. | fix | analytics | 4 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **13** | audit-intake | open (PIB-P08 P0; promoted from PROCESS_INTELLIGENCE_BENCHMARK_REVIEW_001 §9; analytics F2; userPlan race) |
| ~~95~~ | ~~**PIB-P09** Chip-click rate denominator~~ — **done (2026-09-24 loop 46).** `dashboard_v2_viewed` now carries `chipsRenderedCount: number`, populated from `insightChips.length` at emit. **Verified rather than assumed, because the field is only correct if state-count equals screen-count:** `InsightsStrip` hides dismissed chips, but `dismissedIds` is component state initialised empty on mount and this event fires once per mount, so no dismissal can have occurred yet; the strip is additionally suppressed in first-run and error states, and in both the chip array is necessarily empty (every chip derives from workflows via `>= 2`-style thresholds — pinned by a test that `computeInsightChips([], [])` returns `[]` — and the error path never populates them). +5 tests: type acceptance, a `@ts-expect-error` proving the field is mandatory so it cannot be silently dropped, a source assertion that the emit is wired to the rendered array, a no-content check, and the zero-workflows premise. **The field alone does NOT close the launch criterion** — `PRD_METRICS_ENGINE_REVISED.md` §16 criterion 3 still divides by *sessions that viewed the dashboard*, which includes sessions where no chip was on screen. The computable denominator is annotated there with three options, **left for CEO ruling because adopting it makes the 10% bar easier to clear** and that is a launch-gate change. Original text: **PIB-P09** Chip-click rate denominator (`chipsRenderedCount` in `dashboard_v2_viewed`) — current `dashboard_v2_viewed` event lacks `chipsRenderedCount` field, so chip-click rate computation has unstable denominator across users with different chip counts (gated/ungated, plan tier variations). Fix: extend `dashboard_v2_viewed` AnalyticsEvent variant with `chipsRenderedCount: number` populated at view-emit time; downstream PostHog query uses `chipsRenderedCount` as denominator. ~10 LOC + 4-6 tests. The consult **rejects** per-step "observed in this recording" (true on every step by construction at N=1 — repetition, not signal) and flags `"Actual time"` as comparison-flavoured. **Blocked on:** either cross-run SOP generation (a real feature, not a rendering change) or acceptance of the N=1 cut above. | ~~228~~ | ~~**The E2E auth setup is still flaky**~~ — **CLOSED loop 59 by removing the failure class rather than guarding it.** The database file is **no longer deleted**. `global-setup.ts` used to `fs.unlinkSync` it before seeding; Playwright reuses an existing server locally, so that could strand a live server holding the old inode — it kept serving a deleted file, every login failed silently (`auth.ts` returns `null` for a bad password and for this alike), and the only symptom was a 60s `waitForURL` timeout that blocked the entire authenticated project. **Now the seed clears every table IN PLACE** — enumerated from `sqlite_master` so a new model cannot silently escape the reset — and `prisma db push` reconciles the schema in the same file. A stranded server keeps its handle and simply sees fresh data. **The guard went with the deletion that motivated it**, since loop 58 proved no port check can distinguish Playwright's own webServer from a foreign one. Deleting the `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars is gone too — removing those from under a live connection is a documented way to corrupt SQLite. **Proven, not asserted: three consecutive back-to-back runs, 2/2 each, zero flaky** — the exact sequence that previously produced one flake and then a hard failure of both setups leaving 15 tests unrun. Then 21/21 across the dashboard and SOP a11y suites, confirming the clear-and-reseed produces correct data. Bonus: the seed script is now re-runnable by hand. Original text: **DIAGNOSED at loop 58 — root cause located, one hypothesis disproved, one fix built and REJECTED. No code shipped, deliberately.** **(1) Rate limiting is NOT the cause — disproved, not assumed.** The login limiter is 10/15min keyed by IP, and every local test login derives `'unknown'`, so a shared exhausted bucket looked like an excellent explanation. It is wrong: `auth-buckets.ts:53` short-circuits `if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'test') return { allowed: true }`, and `playwright.config.ts:107` sets exactly that. My vitest proof of the mechanism failed for the same reason, which is how the hypothesis died. **(2) The real defect, located precisely:** `global-setup.ts:39` treats a 2-second HTTP-probe **timeout** as *"nothing is listening"* before deleting `prisma/test.db`. A server that IS listening but is busy — exactly the back-to-back run this guard exists to catch — cannot answer `/api/health` in 2s, so the probe concludes "no server", deletes the database, and strands the very server it was checking for. The resulting login failure is silent and indistinguishable from a wrong password (`auth.ts:34` returns `null`), which is why it presents as a 60s `waitForURL` timeout. **This is the same error class as the loop-54 proxy-hops bug: an ambiguous signal resolved toward the dangerous interpretation.** **(3) A fix was built, proven, and then rejected.** Replacing the HTTP probe with a TCP connect was demonstrated to work — against a socket that accepts connections but never answers HTTP, the old probe returns `false` (would delete) and the TCP check returns `true` (refuses). **But it false-positives against Playwright's OWN webServer**, which is already bound to 3098 by the time `globalSetup` runs, so it blocked every legitimate local run. Reverted. The old probe's leniency turns out to be accidentally load-bearing. **"Is the port open" cannot distinguish our server from a foreign one, so no port check can fix this.** **(4) The correct fix, specified but not implemented:** stop deleting the file. Reset the data **in place** — preserving the inode — so a server still holding the handle keeps serving the same file and simply sees fresh data. That removes the entire failure class rather than guarding against it, and needs no ability to tell our server from anyone else's. **(5) Corroboration:** a genuinely stranded server WAS found bound to 3098 after back-to-back runs, and the flake reproduced again after the revert (`1 flaky`). A cold, isolated run passes 2/2, which is why this never appears in isolation. Original text: **The E2E auth setup is still flaky, and #214 was struck as fixed** — observed twice in one session at loop 57 while running a11y specs: once as `1 flaky` (`free-auth.setup.ts` retried and passed) and once as a hard failure of **both** `auth.setup.ts` and `free-auth.setup.ts`, leaving **15 tests unrun**. A retry then passed 17/17, so the specs themselves are sound. **Different symptom from the one loop 37 fixed:** that added a fail-fast guard for a server already listening on 3098; this fails later, at `free-auth.setup.ts:31 waitForURL('**/dashboard**')` timing out after 60s — a login that never completes rather than a port conflict. Likely candidates, none verified: DB contention between back-to-back runs, first-compile slowness exceeding the 60s budget on a cold Next build, or residual state from the prior run's server. **Why it matters beyond local annoyance:** these setups gate the entire `authenticated` project in the deploy pipeline, so a hard failure blocks a deploy with no relation to the change being deployed. Reproduce by running two Playwright invocations back to back. | fix | web-app / qa | 3 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 3 | 2 | **9** | 2026-09-29 L57 | **done (loop 59)** |

Two were already fixed at loop 62 and are not in this row: `text-amber-600` on `bg-amber-50` measured **3.07:1** against a 4.5 requirement (now amber-700, 4.84:1), and `SOPHeader` put the theme-dependent `--brand-text` on a **hardcoded light** `bg-brand-50` chip, which in dark theme resolves to `#34D399` for **1.82:1** (now `text-brand-700`, 5.21:1) — that second one is row #223's class exactly. **Fix these together with the three omitted tests in `sop-a11y.spec.ts`, in one commit**, so the coverage and the clean result land together rather than a ratchet being raised to accommodate them.~~ **CLOSED loop 63. All three fixed, plus two latent siblings the scan could not see: the confidence-colour triple was duplicated in five files and failed as text in one theme or the other at EVERY band (green 4.29 dark / 3.77 light, blue 3.13 dark, amber 3.19 light), so fixing only the band the fixture happened to render would have left two behind. Ring uses `--brand-text`, not the house emerald-500, which is 2.42:1 in light theme — see #230.** | fix | web-app / a11y | 4 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 1 | **13** | 2026-09-30 L62 | closed |
| 231 | **Flow View: per-step detail is hover-only, and the scrollbar is hardcoded light.** Two defects in `SOPVisualMode.tsx` deliberately left out of #229 to keep that change to one outcome; both raised by a11y-architect. **(1) SC 1.4.13** — each step dot carries its detail in a `title` attribute and a `group-hover:opacity-100` label. Neither fires on `:focus`. #229 made the strip a tab stop, so a keyboard user can now scroll it, but still gets nothing per-step; the container's summary `aria-label` remains the only channel. The architect's note is that this was never reachable, so #229 took nothing away — but it is the real remaining gap in the component. Suggested direction: `role="list"` + `role="listitem"` per dot with its own `aria-label` mirroring the title, which also supersedes the `group` role #229 settled on. **(2)** `style={{ scrollbarColor: '#e2e8f0 transparent' }}` is a hardcoded light-grey scrollbar on a dark-default theme — not a WCAG failure (no SC governs scrollbar colour) but the same theme-token defect class as #223. Ship as two commits or one; they are independent. | improvement | web-app / a11y | 3 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 2 | 1 | **10** | 2026-09-30 L63 | open |
| ~~272~~ | ~~**An admin can make someone a team owner by inviting them — bypassing the rule that only an owner can grant owner.** Found at loop 94 (#261), verified by the coordinator in code. `PATCH /api/teams/[id]/members/[memberId]` enforces `role` ∈ {owner, admin, member, viewer} **and** "only an owner can promote to owner" (`forbidden_role_elevation`). `POST /api/teams/[id]/invite` lets owners **and admins** invite, and stores `body.role` **unchecked** (`invite/route.ts` ~94); `invites/accept` then writes `invite.role` into the membership. So an admin can invite an address they control as `owner` and accept it — privilege escalation past an explicit guard — and any caller with invite rights can persist an arbitrary role string, which every `role === …` check downstream will then treat as "none of the above". **Fix:** validate `role` against the same role set; apply the same elevation rule as the PATCH route (only an owner may invite as owner; consider whether an admin may invite as admin); add tests for both, and check `invites/accept` for stored invites that already carry an invalid or elevated role (counts only). Related, lower risk: `workflows/[id]/share` `permission` accepts any string.~~ — **CLOSED loop 95 (`security-reviewer`; coordinator re-verified, build included).** One source of truth, `lib/team-roles.ts` (role set, `isTeamRole`, `isRoleElevation`), now used by both the role-change route and the invite route; the role-change route's behaviour, codes and messages are unchanged. **Rule:** role ∈ {owner, admin, member, viewer}; only an active owner may grant `owner`; an admin may grant admin/member/viewer — exactly what role change has always allowed. Invite: invalid role → 400 (field named, value never echoed); `owner` from a non-owner → 403 `forbidden_role_elevation`. **Defence in depth at acceptance, for invites created before this fix:** `invites/accept` re-checks the stored role inside its transaction and **refuses** (403, writes nothing) an invalid role, or an `owner` invite whose inviter is not *currently* an active owner. Refuse rather than downgrade, deliberately: a downgrade silently grants a role nobody asked for, and checking the inviter's current role cannot escalate. **Matrix tested:** 5 callers × 6 requested roles = 30 invite cases, plus 10 acceptance cases; mutation-checked on both the invite elevation rule and the acceptance check. **Residual, class-scoped: paths that can write a team role outside the set or above the actor's authority, 3 → 0** (invite upsert; acceptance, both resurrect and create), every other writer enumerated and legitimate (team creation and the billing webhook assign `owner` to the creator). `WorkflowShare.permission` checked: written, never read anywhere — inert, left alone and noted. Remaining exposure filed as #273.| fix | security / authz | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 1 | 1 | **16** | 2026-10-01 L94 | closed |
| ~~274~~ | ~~**A team admin can remove every owner, including the last one.** AUTHZ_AUDIT_001 P1-3. **[Corrected at MR-047: the coordinator re-graded this to P0 at intake; MR-045 rejected the mechanism and the auditor (00f4120) graded it P1 — blast radius one team. It is P1.]** ~~re-graded to P0 at intake on coordinator verification~~ (the audit's own P0 definition: exploitable escalation; this one has no mitigating condition) — MR-045 asked to ratify or reject the re-grade. `DELETE /api/teams/[id]/members/[memberId]` (and the bulk removal in `members/route.ts:146-187`) lets an owner **or admin** remove a member without checking the *target's* role, so an admin can remove an owner. The sole-owner guard is `db.teamMember.count({ where: { teamId, role: 'owner' } })` with **no `status: 'active'` filter** — and removal only sets `status: 'removed'`, leaving `role: 'owner'` on the row — so removed owners still count, and an admin can remove owners one at a time down to zero active owners. The role-change `PATCH` likewise never checks the target's role (an admin can demote an owner, subject to the same miscounting guard). Same class as #272: an action an admin is not authorised to take, reachable by another route. **Fix:** in all three paths, a non-owner may not remove or change the role of an owner (403 `forbidden_role_elevation`, matching #272), using `lib/team-roles.ts`; count owners with `status: 'active'`; test the caller × target-role matrix for remove and role-change; check every other `role: 'owner'` count in the codebase for the same missing filter.~~ — **CLOSED loop 97 (`security-reviewer`; coordinator re-verified, build included).** New `isActionOnHigherAuthority` in `lib/team-roles.ts`, used by single remove, bulk remove and role change: **a non-owner may not remove or change the role of an owner** (403 `forbidden_role_elevation`, the code loop 95 uses); role change now checks the target's *current* role as well as the new one. Admins act on admins as peers — consistent with the invite rule, which lets an admin grant `admin`. The last-owner guards now count only **active** owners and apply only when the target is an active owner — the miscount that let removed rows stand in for real ones is gone; the last active owner still cannot remove or demote themselves. **Tested against a stateful in-memory table so the real status filter runs:** caller (5) × target (4) × path (3) = 60 cells plus the miscount and last-owner cases (67 tests); only admin → owner is newly refused, and the owner row is asserted untouched. Mutation-checked: dropping the target-role check fails 4; reverting the status filter fails 3. **Residual, class-scoped: paths by which an actor can reduce the authority of someone above them or reach zero active owners, 3 → 0**; every writer of `TeamMember.role`/`status` enumerated. Left: a concurrent-removal race, #275.| fix | security / authz | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 1 | 1 | **16** | MR-045-promoted (was: audit-intake re-graded by coordinator — mechanism rejected at MR-045) | closed |
| ~~276~~ | ~~**Any signed-in user can very likely make themselves an admin in production, and admin can read every user's email.** AUTHZ_AUDIT_001 P1-1, promoted by MR-045 as a P0 candidate. `POST /api/admin/bootstrap` sets `isAdmin: true` on the caller if no user is an admin yet; four surfaces gate on that flag rather than on the admin allowlist (`analytics/events` GET, `analytics/engagement` — which returns every user's email — `analytics/retention`, `(app)/analytics/product/page.tsx`). **The only guard, `DISABLE_ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP`, never reaches the app:** `deploy.yml:173` sets it, but `compose.hostinger.yaml` does not pass it to the container, and its own comment says unlisted variables never arrive. The coordinator wrote at intake that this was "closed in production" — **that was wrong**, and was caught by MR-045, not by the coordinator. Whether it is exploitable today depends on whether production already has an `isAdmin` row. **Fix in code, so no variable can reopen it:** bootstrap refuses any caller not on the admin allowlist (or is retired); the four surfaces gate on the allowlist; one admin definition. Residual: admin surfaces gated on `isAdmin` instead of the allowlist, 4 → 0, plus bootstrap. **CEO checks:** which users in production have `isAdmin = true`, and since when.~~ — **CLOSED loop 98 (`security-reviewer`; coordinator re-verified, build included) — fixed in code, independent of any environment variable.** **Bootstrap retired:** `/api/admin/bootstrap` is now a stub that always answers 410 — no auth, no database, no variable read, nothing written — because nothing called it and `isAdmin` no longer confers authority, so a promotion would have had nothing to do. | 291 | **The per-account login throttle can be reset or switched off by other traffic, and is ignored by parallel requests.** MR-049 §3.2–3.3; a defect in code shipped at loop 107 (#289). (1) **Saturation:** `trim()` evicts only *unlocked* entries (`account-throttle.ts:69-74`). Once ~50,000 junk addresses are locked — cheap: non-existent accounts skip bcrypt (`auth.ts:50-52`) and rotating `X-Forwarded-For` defeats the IP limit — the address being guessed is evicted on its own next failure and never counts past one. Executed: 905 of 1,000 sequential guesses evaluated, against 10 without the flood. The same eviction resets a partly-counted admin entry. Locked entries are retained, so memory is not bounded as the header claims, and each failure at the cap scans the map twice (~1.3 ms). (2) **Concurrency:** check (`auth.ts:47`) → awaited bcrypt (`:55`) → record; every request that passes the check before the first failure is recorded is evaluated (200 of 200). Fix shape: never evict the key being written and never let a full table stop counting (e.g. fixed-size hashed counters — a collision only over-throttles); reserve the attempt before the compare and refund on success; correct the header and the CEO-facing lines. Tests: the three §3.3 scenarios plus a parallel burst. | 290 | **The login throttle can be used to keep an admin out, and login timing still reveals whether an account exists.** Left by loop 107 (#289). (1) **Accepted trade-off, recorded so it is a decision and not a surprise:** anyone who knows an admin address can fail five guesses and hold it in a refusal window of up to 15 minutes, repeatedly, at about four requests an hour — and during a refusal even the correct password is refused (that is what makes the throttle work). Mitigations to consider: email the account owner when a lock trips (so the attack is visible), and/or a known-device bypass. (2) **Timing oracle, pre-existing:** a non-existent account skips the bcrypt compare, so response time can reveal whether an address is registered. Fix: compare against a fixed dummy hash when the user does not exist. (3) The throttle is in-process — durable or shared storage would be needed if production runs more than one instance. **(4) Added at MR-049 — a forgot-password timing oracle, louder than (2):** for an existing account the route awaits two DB writes and `sendEmail` (`forgot-password/route.ts:79`, up to the 20 s SMTP deadline); for a non-existent one it returns at `:56`. Fix: respond before sending (send detached) or equalise. The dummy hash in (2) also raises the cost of #291's flood.| fix | security / authz | 3 | 4 | 2 | 4 | 2 | 1 | **10** | 2026-10-02 L107 | open |

| ~~266~~ | ~~**A permanently broken second alert channel stays invisible, and the manual alert endpoint reports "sent" regardless of delivery.** Left by loop 90 (#263), by stated choice. (1) When an alert reaches one channel and another fails, `alerts/check` answers 200 with a `console.warn` only — so a revoked Slack webhook alongside working email is never surfaced to the hourly job, and the redundancy the operator configured silently decays to one channel. (2) `POST /api/admin/alerts` ignores the new delivery outcomes and returns `sent: N` even when nothing was delivered — an admin sees success. **Fix:** surface per-channel failure as a distinct, non-failing signal the job can label (or a separate weekly check), and make the manual endpoint return the real outcome counts. **(3), added at MR-043:** the SMTP transport has no timeout (`lib/email.ts:65`), so a hung email server is reported by the hourly job as "unreachable" (exit 3) rather than as an undelivered alert (424).~~ — **CLOSED loop 102 (`backend-engineer`; coordinator re-verified incl. the script under a no-network stub).** **(1) Partial channel failure → HTTP 207**, and the hourly job fails on it (exit 6) with its own message: every firing alert reached someone, but a configured channel is broken. 207 because no framework or proxy emits it, so the job can label it from the status alone (public logs). Fail rather than warn, deliberately: GitHub Actions has no warning state, and a log-only warning is how a revoked webhook decays silently until the second channel breaks too. Precedence 500/503/401 > 424 > 207 > 200; 207 is not a server fault and is not reported. **(2)** The manual send endpoint reports real outcomes (`alertsDelivered`, `alertsUndelivered`, per-channel counts, 424 when an alert reached nobody) instead of `sent: N` regardless. **(3) SMTP** gets connection/greeting/socket timeouts plus a 20 s hard deadline (the per-phase timeouts can stack past the job's 30 s budget), so a hung server is a failed channel (424/207) instead of "unreachable"; this also bounds password-reset email. **Residual, class-scoped: alert delivery failures invisible to the job or the sending admin, 5 → 0 of those closable here.** Left and filed: a broken channel during a quiet hour (#282).| fix | infra / monitoring | 3 | 4 | 2 | 4 | 2 | 1 | **10** | 2026-10-01 L90 | closed |

The drains are: batch flush at >=10 events (unreachable for one event), `visibilitychange` -> hidden, and `beforeunload` (excluded by premise — it is why #241 and #242 exist). **So the bounce leaves the browser only if a drain fires strictly after `pagehide`.** Leaving a visible page works: `pagehide`, then hidden, then delivered. **Background-then-discard does not:** hidden fires *first* and drains an empty buffer, `pagehide` arrives later on freeze, and no further transition occurs. Measured on `#F8FAFC`: **green-400 1.84, red-400 2.64, amber-400 1.60** — all three under even the 3:1 icon floor, let alone 4.5 for text. On tints: red-400 on `bg-red-500/15` = **2.18**; amber-400 on `bg-amber-500/15` = **1.43**; amber-400 on `bg-amber-900/25` = **1.04**, i.e. invisible. **~36 lines** across `dashboard-v2`, `admin-operations`, `demo`, `seo` and `sop-view`, spanning text, badges, `AlertTriangle` icons and `fill-amber-400` favourite stars. **The blind spot is written down in the code:** `WorkflowRow.tsx:263` carries *"text-red-400 (#f87171) ≈ 6.1:1 on dark surface (#0D1117) — WCAG AA compliant"* — correct, and computed for one theme only. **Fix:** the per-theme `--status-success` / `--status-danger` / `--status-warning` tokens already exist and already pass both themes (4.79 / 4.62 / 4.80 light) — this is reaching for them, not inventing anything. The tinted backgrounds need per-theme treatment too, not just the foreground. **Then add the light-theme dashboard test** loop 64 deliberately withheld rather than ship red; `e2e/helpers/theme.ts` and the pattern are already in `sop-a11y.spec.ts`. Extending light coverage to the public pages is the same job.~~ **CLOSED loop 67. The held dashboard light test is shipped and green — which is the point: a hold-back convention is worth nothing unless holds actually return.** ~36 foreground sites swapped to the per-theme status tokens across 21 files (`frontend-engineer`). Backgrounds deliberately untouched: the health rails are `aria-hidden="true"`, confirmed by the existing 1.4.1/1.4.11 lock test, so they are decorative and exempt. **The scope was wrong again, and the test found what the grep did not** — the held test failed on `text-green-600`, a family this row never mentioned. `TopBand` and `SignalFactsRow` render the delta with `text-green-600` / `text-red-600` on the bare theme surface, and they fail in **opposite** themes: 3.15 light for green, 3.35 dark for red. **The dark one has been live in the DEFAULT theme the whole time, with a full dark-theme axe ratchet in place**, because no fixture had ever set `portfolioHealthScoreDelta` — the delta simply never rendered. Both now use the tokens. Residual filed as **#236**: the translucent-tint badges improved hugely (1.04 → 3.12, 1.43 → 4.30) but still do not clear 4.5 in light, because the tint itself is a hardcoded dark-theme background. | fix | web-app / a11y | 4 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 4 | 2 | **10** | 2026-09-30 L64 | closed |
| ~~240~~ | ~~**The "add to favourites" star is invisible until hover, and there is no hover on touch.** Surfaced by the row-#239 guard: `dashboard/page.tsx:1523,1768` render the unfilled star as `text-[var(--border-default)]` with `group-hover:text-[var(--content-tertiary)]`. `--border-default` is `#E2E8F0` in light theme — **1.18:1** against the surface, i.e. effectively not drawn. On a mouse it appears on row hover and the interaction works. **On a touch device there is no hover state**, so the affordance is never revealed and the feature is undiscoverable. This is a discoverability defect rather than a contrast one — an always-visible-but-quiet star is the usual answer (`--content-tertiary` is 7.24:1 light / 5.52 dark and already per-theme), with the filled state carrying the distinction. Excluded from #239's guard with this row cited, because the contrast floor is the wrong frame for it: a genuinely decorative element may sit at 1.18:1, and this one is not decorative. Check the same pattern on the workflow detail page (`workflows/[id]/page.tsx:338`), which uses `--content-tertiary` already and may simply be the correct version of this. **SHARPENED loop 72 — it is worse than filed, and the worst case is mobile.** `dashboard/page.tsx` renders `WorkflowRow` twice. **Desktop (`:1519`)** has `text-[var(--border-default)] group-hover:text-[var(--content-tertiary)]` — invisible until hover, which at least works with a mouse. **Mobile/tablet (`:1764`)** has `text-[var(--border-default)]` and **no hover reveal at all**. So the star is invisible on exactly the devices that can never reveal it: not an edge case, the entire touch experience. **The correct version already exists** — `workflows/[id]/page.tsx:338` uses `text-[var(--content-tertiary)]` unconditionally (7.24:1 light / 5.52:1 dark, per-theme, already guarded). This is therefore a consistency fix with a known-good reference, not a design question. Keep the filled state as-is so favourited vs not stays obvious; the hover transition on desktop can stay as an emphasis on top of a visible baseline rather than as the thing that makes it exist.~~ **CLOSED loop 74.** Both dashboard layouts now use `--content-tertiary`, matching `workflows/[id]/page.tsx`, which already had it right — 7.24:1 light / 5.52:1 dark, per-theme, and already covered by the contrast guard. Desktop keeps a hover transition, but as *emphasis on something visible* rather than as the thing that makes it exist. **The allowlist entry that had been covering this is now counted**, which is the more durable half of the fix: it exempted two decorative `&#124;` separators in the same file and was silently extending that exemption to a favourite-star affordance at 1.18:1 on devices with no hover. An allowlist keyed by file grants a reason written for one thing to everything that follows it. It now declares how many uses it covers, and a third use fails with "a new use has inherited an exemption written for something else" — sabotage-verified. | fix | web-app / ux | 3 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 1 | **11** | 2026-09-30 L69 | closed |
| ~~239~~ | ~~**`--accent` is #20f2a6 with no `.light` override, so it is 1.40:1 in light theme wherever it carries meaning.**~~ **CLOSED loop 69 (MR-037 S-1).** Loop 65 measured it at 1.40:1 and called it the worst value in the application; loop 68 then ran a 73-site colour audit, edited three lines carrying it, and measured none — because its instrument asked a *syntactic* question (does this `var()` name the token it resolves to?) while the defect is *semantic* (is the resolved colour readable?). **Fixed the two that are text:** the admin dashboard's **MRR figure** at 20px (`AdminOperationsDashboard.tsx:725`) and `KpiTile.tsx:30`, both → `--brand-text` (per-theme, 8.42 dark / 5.24 light, already asserted). The third, `LensSwitcher.tsx:129`, is an active-tab border sitting on its own rgba fill, so the page surface is not its neighbour — recorded in the guard's allowlist with that reason rather than changed. **The structural fix is the guard**, extended from syntax to semantics: every `var(--X)` in a `text-` or `ring-` position is now resolved and checked against its floor in both themes. Deliberately NOT `border-` or `fill-`: a first draft included them and reported hundreds of hairline dividers at ~1.1:1, which SC 1.4.11 does not require and which would have trained people to ignore the file. | fix | web-app / a11y | 4 | 4 | 4 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **13** | 2026-09-30 L69 | closed |
| ~~244~~ | ~~**Funnel stage 2 was widened without stage 1, so `plan_limit_hit → upgrade_prompt_viewed` will read over 100% for a new reason.** MR-038 §3.4. Row #238 made the quota chip emit `upgrade_prompt_viewed` whenever its CTA renders, which is from **80%** of quota (`quota-meter.ts` `QUOTA_WARNING_PCT`). `plan_limit_hit` still only fires on an actual rejection at 100%. So prompts now legitimately exceed limit-hits. **This matters because of what the CHANGELOG told the CEO:** that a >100% reading in this funnel was the *old* undercount defect. If the number goes back over 100% for this new and benign reason, that guidance points at the wrong cause. **Fix:** either emit a warning-tier `plan_limit_hit` at the same threshold the prompt uses, or separate the warning prompt from the at-limit prompt by `location` so the stages line up per surface — the latter is consistent with #238's own ruling that these stages should be compared per `location` rather than in aggregate. Then correct the CHANGELOG guidance.~~ **CLOSED loop 78 — the stage-1-to-2 half.** The warning-tier prompt (from 80%) and the at-limit prompt (100%) now report **different locations**, so the funnel can compare like with like; `plan_limit_hit` only fires server-side when an action is actually blocked, so lumping them together made stage 2 legitimately exceed stage 1. The at-limit case keeps the original location so historical click data stays comparable, and the **click reports the same location as the view** — a view and a click filed under different names is this row's defect wearing different clothes. **MR-039 found the stage-2-to-3 half is also broken and is NOT covered here:** `upgrade_clicked` includes the pricing-page button, for which no view is emitted by design, and `computeFunnel` has no `location` dimension at all — so the per-location comparison this row relies on cannot actually be performed. Filed as #248. | improvement | web-app / analytics | 3 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 1 | **12** | 2026-09-30 L73 | closed |
| ~~243~~ | ~~**The analytics ingest route abandons the rest of a batch on the first failure and still reports success.** `api/analytics/events/route.ts:41-48` wraps the whole insert loop in one `try`, so one bad row discards every event after it — and the route returns `{ ok: true, received: <full count> }` regardless, so the client is told everything landed. Non-directional rather than flattering, but it silently truncates batches, which is the same family as #238/#241/#242 and the same shape as a defect already fixed once at `01aef52`. **Fix:** per-row try, count actual inserts, and report the real number. **Also here, latent and stated as such:** `:32` does `events.slice(0, 100)` with no error and no log, so a batch over 100 is silently truncated. I could not construct a realistic path past 100 given the 10-event flush threshold, so this is recorded at its true weight rather than inflated — but a truncation that says nothing is exactly what the other three rows were.~~ **CLOSED loop 75.** Each row now gets its own `try`, so one bad event costs one event rather than the tail of the batch, and the response reports what was actually **written** rather than what was built — `received` now means received. Added `attempted`, `failed` and `truncated`, so a truncated batch says so instead of vanishing; the 100-row cap stays, since an unbounded batch is a denial-of-service shape. The outer catch no longer returns `ok: true` over a total failure. **The request still returns HTTP 200 regardless** — analytics must never surface as a broken page — and that is the distinction worth naming: an honest body and a benign status are separable, and conflating them is exactly what produced a success report over a partial write. 5 tests, sabotage-verified: restoring the single-`try` shape and the built-not-written count fails four of them. | fix | web-app / analytics | 3 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 1 | 2 | **12** | 2026-09-30 L73 | closed |
| ~~242~~ | ~~**`dashboard_bounced` is emitted from `beforeunload` — the trigger loop 71 spent a whole loop condemning — so on mobile the bounce is never produced at all.** Found by MR-038 inspection, and it is the third member of the flattering-undercount family after #238 and #241. `DashboardV2Shell.tsx:741` registers the emitter on `window.addEventListener('beforeunload', …)`. `analytics-delivery.ts:10-12`, written one loop earlier, states that `beforeunload` *"is unreliable by design on mobile. iOS Safari and Chrome on Android routinely discard a page without firing it."* **Loop 71 fixed transport and left emission on the trigger it had just declared unreliable** — so there is nothing in the buffer for the new delivery path to deliver. **The bias is the flattering one at maximum leverage:** a bounce not emitted is a bounce that did not happen, and the population whose page teardown skips `beforeunload` is disproportionately the population that bounced. **It is criterion 1 of the #57 retirement rule (`bounce < 40%`)**, so two of that decision's three criteria are now unsound — criterion 3 visibly (unscoreable 24 loops) and criterion 1 invisibly. **Fix:** `pagehide`, which is reliable on mobile including bfcache and, unlike `visibilitychange`, does not fire on a tab switch — a bounce is about leaving, not about looking away. **Delete the `shouldEmitBounce` / `computeBounceElapsedMs` mirrors in `DashboardV2Shell.test.tsx:435,445` in the same commit**: nine green tests sit directly over this defect and are blind to it precisely because the trigger is the part that was never extracted.~~ **CLOSED loop 73.** Emitter moved to `pagehide`. **`visibilitychange` was the tempting wrong answer** — row #241 used it correctly for *delivery*, and reaching for it again here would have counted every tab switch as leaving, swapping an undercount for an overcount. `pagehide` means navigating away, including into bfcache, and is reliable on mobile. **The nine mirrored tests are gone:** `DashboardV2Shell.test.tsx` defined its own copies of the predicate under a docstring saying "Logic mirrors DashboardV2Shell's handleBeforeUnload", and they now import `lib/bounce.ts` instead. **The trigger is exported from that module specifically so it is part of the tested surface** — the predicate was never wrong, and testing it nine times against a copy could never have found this. Three unit assertions pin the trigger *with the reasoning*, including that it is not `beforeunload` and not `visibilitychange`. **Verified in a browser**, which is the only place a page-lifecycle event is real: leaving without interacting produces exactly one bounce; leaving after a click produces none. Sabotage-verified by reverting the trigger. **The second browser test failed first for the wrong reason** — the click landed on an ancestor's padding rather than a descendant of the shell root the capture listener is on, so it reported a bounce the product would also have reported: right answer, wrong reason, and recorded in the file. **#57 criterion 1 (`bounce < 40%`) is now measurable on mobile**; criterion 3 remains unscoreable. | fix | web-app / analytics | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **14** | 2026-09-30 L73 | closed |
| ~~241~~ | ~~**Analytics events are delivered in batches of ten, with the remainder relying on `beforeunload` — which mobile browsers frequently never fire.** Found at loop 70 while building #238's verification. `lib/analytics.ts` buffers to `window.__ledgerium_events` and only calls `flushEvents()` once **`buffer.length >= 10`**; anything below that is delivered by a `sendBeacon` on `beforeunload`. A typical dashboard visit produces two or three events. **`beforeunload` is unreliable by design on mobile** — iOS Safari and Chrome on Android routinely discard a page without firing it, and the platform guidance is to use `visibilitychange` → `hidden` instead, which is the last event a page is guaranteed to see. So an unknown share of short sessions is being dropped entirely, and **short sessions are not a random sample** — they correlate with bouncing, which is exactly the behaviour the funnel is meant to measure. This is the second defect in a fortnight where an analytics gap biased a number in a flattering direction (#238 was the first). **Fix:** flush on `visibilitychange` → `hidden` in addition to `beforeunload`, and consider lowering or removing the batch threshold for conversion events specifically. **Verify by measuring**, not by reasoning: compare `dashboard_v2_viewed` counts against server-side page requests for the same window, and the shortfall is the loss rate.~~ **CLOSED loop 71.** Delivery now happens on `visibilitychange` → `hidden`, which is the last event a page is guaranteed to see; `beforeunload` is kept as a desktop fallback. **A second defect turned up in the reading and had to be fixed first:** the old unload handler read the buffer **without clearing it**. That was survivable only because the page was about to be destroyed — and it stops being survivable the moment a visibility trigger exists, since that fires on every tab switch, app switch and screen lock. Without draining, a user who switches away five times would have sent the same events six times. **Trading lost events for duplicated events is not a fix**, so every delivery path now goes through one `drain`-and-send function that makes read-without-clear impossible. Also handled: a refused `sendBeacon` (browsers cap queued bytes) puts the events back rather than dropping them, since losing data while fixing data loss would be a poor outcome. Rule in `lib/analytics-delivery.ts` (13 tests incl. the hide→return→hide sequence); wiring verified in a browser by stubbing `sendBeacon` and driving real visibility transitions, and sabotage-verified. **Batch threshold left at 10** — the lifecycle path is now reliable, so lowering it would add request volume for no correctness gain. | fix | web-app / analytics | 4 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 2 | 2 | **11** | 2026-09-30 L70 | closed |
| ~~238~~ | ~~**The conversion funnel's "upgrade prompt shown" step counts one of four surfaces, so its click-through rate is inflated — possibly above 100%.** Found at loop 68 while tracing #235. `analytics/product/page.tsx:332` defines the funnel as `['plan_limit_hit', 'upgrade_prompt_viewed', 'upgrade_clicked', 'checkout_started', 'subscription_created']`. **`upgrade_clicked` fires from four live surfaces** — `teams_create` (`teams/page.tsx`), `dashboard_v2_quota_chip` (`RecordingQuotaChip.tsx:52`), `dashboard_v2_health_gate` (`WorkflowRow.tsx:346`), `upgrade_button` (`UpgradeButton.tsx:47`). **`upgrade_prompt_viewed` fires from exactly one** (`teams/page.tsx:77`). So three of four prompts are shown without being recorded as shown, while every click is recorded. The denominator is missing roughly three-quarters of its impressions, and **the error flatters us** — it makes prompt→click look far better than it is, which is the worst direction for a number used to decide pricing and conversion work. **The fix is already written and unused:** `components/shared/UpgradeCTA.tsx:50` emits `upgrade_prompt_viewed` with a `location` and `plan` on mount, which is exactly right — and nothing renders it (see #235). Either adopt it at the three uncovered surfaces or emit the event where they render their CTA. **Watch for double-counting:** the quota chip and health gate re-render on state changes, so a naive mount-effect would over-count the denominator and swing the error the other way. Fire once per prompt-instance per page view. **Verify against real data afterwards** — if historical prompt→click exceeds 100%, that confirms the defect and dates it.~~ **CLOSED loop 70.** Both in-product prompts now record being shown, with `location` matching their `upgrade_clicked` exactly: `dashboard_v2_quota_chip` (plan `team` — the tier that lifts the recording cap) and `dashboard_v2_health_gate` (plan `starter` — the tier that sets `healthScores: true`, verified in `plans.ts` rather than assumed). **The pricing page's own buttons are deliberately NOT instrumented:** the funnel stage means "the product interrupted someone with an upgrade prompt", and a page the user chose to visit is a destination, not an interruption. That leaves `upgrade_clicked` from `upgrade_button` without a matching view, which is correct — the two stages should be compared per `location`, not in aggregate, and the row now says so. **Counting rule extracted to `lib/upgrade-prompt.ts`** (15 tests) because the real risk in fixing an undercount is causing an overcount: a rule firing per-render would inflate the same denominator, the identical error reversed and no easier to spot. It fires once per prompt instance and again only after the prompt genuinely goes away and returns. **Verified in a browser and sabotage-checked.** **Adjacent fix:** the gated CTA was `text-green-600` = **3.15:1** in light — the defect loop 67 fixed elsewhere, latent here because no fixture renders the gated branch. Now `--status-success`. **#241 filed** for the delivery question found while testing. | fix | web-app / analytics | 5 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **14** | 2026-09-30 L68 | closed |
| 237 | **Fifteen colours are hardcoded literals that were meant to be theme tokens — now visible rather than disguised.** Loop 68 inlined them, because `var(--opp-automate, #2563eb)` where `--opp-automate` is defined nowhere is a literal wearing a token's clothes. The `--opp-*` five are chart fills answering to the 3:1 non-text floor **against each other as well as the surface**, which is a palette problem rather than five independent ones. **Also here:** the light theme has only two distinct surfaces (`--surface-secondary` and `--surface-elevated` are both `#FFFFFF`), so the hover states fixed in #233 work in dark and are no-ops in light — a real depth gap, pre-existing, not introduced. | ~~233~~ | ~~**`var(--X, fallback)` fallbacks across the codebase disagree with the definitions and with each other.** Surfaced by MR-036 via the `LensSwitcher` ring (see #230): a fallback is what the author believed, the definition is what the browser uses, and when they differ anyone measuring the fallback measures fiction. `--accent` is used with a fallback **21 times**, and the fallbacks disagree: **9 sites say `#20f2a6`, 6 say `#16a34a`** — while the actual definition is `#20f2a6` (`globals.css:52`, `:root` only, **no `.light` override**, so it is the same bright mint in both themes and measures **1.40:1** on the light surface). Separately `--accent-subtle` is used with a live fallback `rgba(22,163,74,0.08)` and is **never defined anywhere** — that one silently works, which is worse, because it looks like a token and is not. **Fix:** (a) drop every fallback where the variable is defined — they are dead code that misleads measurement; (b) either define `--accent-subtle` per theme or inline the literal honestly; (c) decide whether `--accent` needs a `.light` value at all, given it is a brand mint that fails contrast on light surfaces wherever it carries meaning. The focus-ring case is already guarded by `theme-contrast.test.ts`; this row is the other ~20 non-ring uses, which nothing checks. Worth extending that guard to all `var()` uses rather than fixing sites one at a time.~~ **CLOSED loop 68, and the row was a third of the real size.** It named `--accent` (21 sites) and `--accent-subtle`. Deriving it found **73 sites across 18 files**, in two distinct defects. **58 dead fallbacks** — a defined token written with a fallback that can never apply: `--content-primary` carried three different fallbacks including `#111827` and `#f1f5f9`, which are opposite themes; `--border-default` carried four; `--surface-primary` and `--surface-elevated` each carried both a light and a dark one. **15 phantom tokens** — `var(--X, literal)` where `--X` is defined nowhere, so the literal is permanently what renders while looking like a theme token: all 5 `--opp-*`, both `--severity-*`, `--content-disabled`, `--accent-subtle`. `band-colors.ts` states the intent in its docstring — *"resolves to the design-system token when defined and to a sensible literal fallback otherwise"* — and **the tokens were never defined**, so that palette has always been its fallbacks. **A live visual bug fell out of it:** `AnalyticsConsent.tsx:67` had `hover:bg-[var(--surface-tertiary)]` with no fallback on an undefined variable, so that button had no hover state at all; `ColumnPicker:351` nested a fallback to `--surface-secondary`, its own base colour, so its hover was invisible too. Both now use `--surface-elevated`. **Every rewrite is provably zero-pixel** — dropping a dead fallback changes nothing because the token already won, and inlining a phantom changes nothing because the fallback was already what rendered. Guarded by `theme-contrast.test.ts`, extended from focus rings to every `var()`; sabotage-verified. | fix | web-app / design-system | 3 | 4 | 4 | 5 | 2 | 1 | **13** | 2026-09-30 L65 | closed |

Worst was `focus-visible:ring-[var(--accent,#20f2a6)]` at **1.40:1** — effectively invisible. | ~~109~~ | ~~**RE-OPENED at loop 61 — I closed this on evidence that did not exist.** Loop 57 closed it by shipping `e2e/app/sop/sop-a11y.spec.ts` and reporting *"4/4 pass, zero critical or serious violations" across all three SOP modes*. **That claim was false and the spec has been removed.** MR-035 flagged the tests as fragile; investigating showed they were **vacuous**: (a) the spec never opened the SOP — `activeTab` is React state initialised to `'workflow'`, so `SOPPageShell` never mounted and every test scanned the default tab; (b) the mode-switch buttons therefore did not exist, and the `if ((await count) > 0)` guard silently skipped, so all three "mode" tests re-scanned the same non-SOP view; (c) the fixture was the wrong shape entirely — the detail route returns `{ workflow, artifacts[] }` and the fixture supplied a flat object, so `artifacts.find(a => a.artifactType === 'sop')` found nothing. **What a correct spec needs, learned the hard way:** click `data-testid="workflow-tab-sop"`; mock `{ workflow, artifacts: [{ artifactType: 'sop', contentJson: {...} }] }`; assert the mode switcher is visible before scanning; switch modes by asserting `aria-pressed="true"` rather than sleeping. Reshaping the fixture to the correct envelope still did not render the SOP, so at least one further requirement remains unidentified — **a seeded workflow with a real SOP artifact is likely a better route than mocking.** **The row's two original ARIA claims remain disproved** (loop 57 verified `role="checkbox"` on a `<button>` is permitted by ARIA-in-HTML, and the `role="listitem"` occurrence does not exist) — that verification stands on its own and is unaffected. Original text: ~~**SOPPM-P03** sop-view ARIA fix + axe regression coverage~~ — **CLOSED loop 57, but NOT as written: both of its "HARD WCAG 2.1 AA violations" were checked before any code was touched and neither survived.** (1) `role="checkbox"` on a `<button>` at `SOPExecutionMode.tsx:566` is a real occurrence, but **ARIA-in-HTML explicitly permits the checkbox role on a button**; paired with `aria-checked` and an accessible name, as it is, that is a supported pattern. Restructuring it would have been churn justified by a false premise. (2) `role="listitem"` on a `<button>` in `AskThisProcessPanel` **does not exist** — that component declares one role in total, `role="alert"`. The only `role="listitem"` occurrences are `<div>`s in `ColumnPicker.tsx`, which does declare `role="list"` ancestors. **What shipped is the row's most valuable ask, which settles the question empirically rather than by argument:** `e2e/app/sop/sop-a11y.spec.ts` ratchets all three SOP modes plus the criteria-checked state. **Run locally against the real app: 4/4 pass, zero critical or serious violations** — so if either claim had been true, these would have failed and named the rule and the node. The axe helper was extracted to `e2e/helpers/axe.ts` rather than copied; `v2-a11y.spec.ts` now imports it and still passes 17/17. | ~~235~~ | ~~**`FeatureGate` is rendered nowhere, so the whole plan-gating component chain is dead code.** Found at loop 66 while tracing #189's call sites: `useAccount` → `useFeatureGate` → `FeatureGate`, and `grep -rn '<FeatureGate'` across `apps/web-app/src` returns **nothing**. MR-036 described `useAccount` as "already written and unused"; it is worse than that — its only consumer's only consumer is itself unrendered. **This matters beyond tidiness:** the product gates features by plan, and if that gating is not going through this component then it is going through something else, somewhere else, and the two may not agree. **Establish which before deleting anything.** Find how plan gating is actually enforced in the UI today, check it against `lib/feature-gating.ts` (which the API route does use), and then either wire `FeatureGate` up where gating is currently ad-hoc, or delete the chain and note what replaced it. `useAccount` itself is now load-bearing after #189 and stays either way.~~ **CLOSED loop 76. The row asked me to establish how gating is really enforced before deleting anything, and that was the right instruction — the answer changed what to delete.** Three mechanisms are live: **(1) server rejects, client explains** — 403 with `code: plan_upgrade_required` + `requiredPlan` + `upgradeUrl`, mapped to a prompt at the point of intent (`api/teams/route.ts`, `teams/createTeamError.ts`); **(2) gated flags in the payload** — `isGated` on values the plan excludes, UI renders a locked state (`canSeeHealthScores`); **(3) admin allowlist**, for admin surfaces only. The product chose attempt-then-reject deliberately: the prompt appears when the user wanted the thing, rather than hiding it in advance. **`FeatureGate` + `useFeatureGate` + `UpgradeCTA` implemented the rejected strategy** — pre-emptive hiding — and were dead: `<FeatureGate` appeared **zero** times in the tree, so the hook and the presentation component were dead behind it. **The dead chain was three files, not two** (the row said two); `UpgradeCTA` was reachable only through `FeatureGate`. All three deleted: a complete, plausible implementation of a rejected strategy is an invitation to adopt it by accident, and a second answer to a question that should have one. **The architecture is now documented in `lib/feature-gating.ts`** — the module that is actually used — because the durable value here is the answer, not the deletion. `useAccount` keeps four real consumers and is unaffected. | improvement | web-app / correctness | 3 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 2 | 2 | **11** | 2026-09-30 L66 | closed |
| ~~234~~ | ~~**`/api/account` is still fetched twice on `/account` and `/upload`, and both parse it as `any`.** #189 fixed the dashboard pair; these two remain. `TrialStatusChip` lives in `AppShell`, so it fetches on **every** app page — each of these pages then fetches again for itself (`account/page.tsx:353` inside a `Promise.all`, `upload/page.tsx:40`). **The stronger reason is correctness, not the duplicate request.** Both read `res.json()` as `any`, and `upload/page.tsx:42` carries a comment describing exactly what that costs: it previously read `data.plan` and `data.uploadCount`, *neither of which exists on that envelope*, so the usage counter and the at-limit lockout **never rendered for anyone** — silently, because `any` defeats typecheck. That is the third instance of this class in one neighbourhood (the others: `TrialStatusChip`'s `daysRemaining` nullability, found at #189). Routing both through the typed `useAccount` closes the class. **The open design question, which is why this is not a blind swap:** `/account` is where a user lands after a plan change, and a 30s TTL could show them their old plan on the one page that exists to display it. Fixed by refetching on mount, and pinned by a test in `accountCache.test.ts` that states the trap explicitly, because the cache is behaving correctly and the lesson is that correct caching is the wrong default for a self-invalidating figure. | fix | web-app / correctness | 3 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **11** | 2026-09-30 L66 | closed |

| 216 | **Status cell corrected at loop 51: it read the bare word `open`, so the blocker existed only in prose.** Every sweep that reads the status field — including my own repeated claim that no unblocked extension row exists — was right by luck rather than by evidence. #107 / #108 / #191 / #225 all carry their blocker in the field; this one did not. Now it does. Original text: **Sensitive-field detection can miss entirely inside shadow DOM** — every content listener inspects `e.target` (`content/capture.ts:137-180`); `composedPath()` appears nowhere in `content/`. For composed events from an open/closed shadow root, `e.target` retargets to the shadow HOST, so `isSensitiveTarget` / `inspectTarget` (`target-inspector.ts:21,139`) classify the host's attributes, not the real `<input type=password>` inside a web-component login or payment widget. Any such site escapes password/payment detection silently. Candidate fix is `e.composedPath()[0]`, **but this changes which element every event is attributed to** — selectors, labels and roles all shift — so it is a capture-semantics change, not a screening tweak: it needs a design decision, the real-extension harness, and CEO awareness under the Extension Reliability Invariant. | 223 | **⚠ SCORE CELL IS NOT MACHINE-READABLE (MR-034, loop 55).** A stray `|` in this row's prose splits its cells, so a positional parser misreads the score — #110 reads as **2** against its true 13. Not corruption: the cell count is identical at loops 50, 51 and HEAD. But any automated ranking silently mis-sorts this row, and eight rows share the defect (#45 #223 #157 #152 #127 #126 #121 #110), several in the score band where selection happens. Escape the literal `|` as `\|` when next editing this row. Original text: **RE-SCOPED at loop 51 — the counts are four months stale.** The row cites 138 / 73 / 153 hardcoded dark-palette occurrences. Live counts are **32 / 35 / 0**. The zero matters: one of the three surfaces is fully done. Re-count before selecting rather than trusting the figures in this row. Original text: **Components hardcode dark-theme colours, so the light theme is unreadable in places** — the remaining light-theme axe nodes after #222 (**landing 11, pricing 26**) are dark-palette values rendering on white: `#34d399 on #ffffff` (9 nodes on pricing), `#10b981 on #ffffff` (6 on landing), plus `#fbbf24`, `#4ade80`, `#f87171`, `#fde68a`. Source is Tailwind classes hardcoded into components — `text-brand-400` (**138 uses**), `text-brand-500` (**73**), `text-amber-400`, `text-red-400`, `text-green-400` — which only work on a dark background. **Partly fixed at loop 35:** `text-[#e2e8f0]` (the dark theme's `--content-primary`, hardcoded **153 times across 40 files**, 134 of them on public pages) was replaced with the token, which took light from 77/83 nodes to 11/26 and is visible in the loop-35 PNGs — body copy that was washed out is now readable. The rest needs a per-class judgement: some of these may sit on deliberately dark surfaces where they are correct. **Closing this unlocks adding the light theme to `e2e/public/a11y.spec.ts`.** **Count corrected at MR-028:** this row said `text-brand-500` 89; it is **73** now. Both figures were right when taken — 89 was measured before loop 35, which converted the 16 `hover:text-brand-500` uses to a token (89 − 16 = 73). Re-measured at MR-028 and again at loop 36 entry. **Loop 36 — public surface done; row stays open for the app surface.** Ruling from the newly-imported `a11y-architect`: keep the token pattern (a literal Tailwind colour class is a hidden per-theme decision that only one theme ever validates), and split the sweep. Its mechanical exception test — walk to the nearest ancestor `bg-*`, and treat literal dark values (`bg-slate-900`, `bg-black`, `bg-[#0…]`) as fixed-dark, everything else as theme-following — resolved cleanly here: **zero** literal-dark backgrounds exist anywhere under `app/(public)/`, so all 157 sites in scope were theme-following and none needed a judgement call. Replaced in 29 files: `text-brand-400` ×106 and `text-brand-500` ×38 → `--brand-text`; `text-amber-400` ×7 + `text-amber-200` ×1 → `--status-warning`; `text-red-400` ×5 → `--status-danger`. **Measured: light landing 11 → 5, pricing 26 → 11; dark stays 0/0.** Cumulative across loops 34-36: **77 → 5** and **83 → 11**. Remaining in-app sites (`text-brand-400` and friends outside `app/(public)/`) are untouched and keep this row open; the architect also flagged two things axe's text rule cannot see — **1.4.11 non-text contrast** (status colours on badges/gauge/chart fills need 3:1, and the token comments only document text ratios) and **1.4.1 use of colour** (a status conveyed by colour alone) — both explicitly unverified. **Loop 38 — both WCAG sub-items resolved as VERIFIED-NOT-VIOLATED, and pinned.** I measured the rail colours first: `bg-red-500` / `bg-amber-500` / `bg-green-500` against a white surface are **3.76 / 2.15 / 2.28:1**, i.e. amber and green sit under the 3:1 non-text floor (all pass on dark: 4.30 / 7.53 / 7.10). `a11y-architect` ruled both **not violations**: the rail is decorative because the verdict WORD carries the same status as visible text beside it, and the wrapper's `aria-label` carries it for AT — so it is not a "graphical object required to understand content" (1.4.11), and colour is never the only channel (1.4.1; row badges are typed `'Healthy' | 'At risk' | 'Needs review'` so text is always present, and their pills carry their own background). It also corrected my input: the verdict word is **also** `aria-hidden`, with the accessible name on the wrapper. **Pinned rather than repainted:** 3 lock tests in `v2-a11y.spec.ts` assert, per band, that the accessible name contains the verdict, that it is visible text, and that the rail keeps `aria-hidden`. They were placed in Playwright, not jsdom, because web-app has neither jsdom nor RTL. **Mutation-tested:** deleting the verdict word makes all 3 fail. | ~~203~~ | ~~**Demo account password is a published default**~~ — **done (2026-09-17 loop 26):** the open question (was production ever seeded with it?) could not be answered from the dev machine, so the fix is the one that is correct either way: `assertDemoCredentialsSafe()` **throws before any database work** when `DEMO_PASSWORD` is the published default AND `DATABASE_URL` is not local (`file:`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`; missing or unparseable **fails closed**). Guard placed before `deleteDemoData` so a refusal cannot half-delete. Error names the remedy, not just the problem. `capture-chrome-store-screenshots.ts` now reads `DEMO_PASSWORD` instead of hardcoding it, so a privately-seeded machine can still capture. Runbook documents the guard and tells anyone who already seeded a remote environment to re-run with a private password (the script is idempotent). **A test caught a real bug in the guard itself:** `[^:/?#]+` stopped at the first colon, so `@[::1]:5432` captured a bare `[` and read as remote; the bracketed-IPv6 alternative now comes first. Verified end to end: default + remote URL exits with the refusal; private password + same URL proceeds past the guard. Previous text: **Demo account password is a published default** — `DEMO_PASSWORD = 'Demo2026!Workspace'` appears in `scripts/capture-chrome-store-screenshots.ts:33`, `scripts/seed-demo-account.ts:44`, its test, `docs/runbooks/DEMO_ACCOUNT_SEED.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`. Deliberate and env-overridable, so not a leak — but any environment seeded with defaults has a publicly known login for `demo@ledgerium.ai`. Decide: require `DEMO_PASSWORD` with no default in non-local environments, or confirm the demo account is disposable by design. **Evidence added loop 21 (coordinator, read-only):** nothing seeds automatically (no CI/deploy reference to `seed:demo`), but `docs/runbooks/DEMO_ACCOUNT_SEED.md:29-31` explicitly tells operators to run the script against the **production** database, and `createDemoUser` gives the account **`plan: 'team'`** (not admin). Whether production was ever seeded cannot be checked from the dev machine. If it was with defaults, anyone reading this repo can log in to a Team-plan account. Impact re-scored 3 → 4 (score 8 → 9). | 225 | **MEASUREMENT SHIPPED loop 53 — the blocking fact is now observable, and the remaining step is reading a number rather than investigating infrastructure.** New `lib/proxy-observation.ts` records how many entries each inbound `x-forwarded-for` carried — **counts only; no address is stored, logged or returned**, and the recording function takes a number so an address cannot be passed by mistake (asserted with `@ts-expect-error`). Surfaced as `data.proxyChain` on `/api/admin/operations`, with `suggestedTrustedProxyHops` alongside `configuredTrustedProxyHops` so the gap between evidence and configuration is visible in one place. **Why the minimum is the right inference:** proxies append and never remove, so no caller can produce a chain SHORTER than the true hop count — the minimum is safe from below, which is the direction that matters; `honestSampleLikely` makes the judgement visible rather than assumed. Runbook at `docs/runbooks/TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS.md`. **Still open** — the variable itself is the CEO's to set, and until it is, login / signup / password-reset limits remain bypassable. Original text: **CENTRALIZED 2026-09-24 (loop 44) — spoofability NOT yet closed, by design. Blocked on one infra fact.** All call sites now route through a single tested helper `lib/client-ip.ts`; **six sites, not the five I filed** — I missed `invites/accept/route.ts:73` (a local `getIp()` carrying a `split(',')[0]!` non-null assertion, now deleted). **Why the obvious fix was not shipped:** `compose.hostinger.yaml` shows the reverse proxy is EXTERNAL and Hostinger-provisioned (*"depending on which proxy Hostinger provisions"*), so the trusted-hop count is not knowable from this repo. Taking the last XFF entry when the real hop count is higher would collapse **every user into one rate-limit bucket** — locking everyone out of login. That availability failure is worse than the spoofability, so the default is unchanged. **Contract:** `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` — unset/blank/non-numeric/negative → `0` → first entry (today's behaviour, byte-identical); `N` → the entry `N` in from the end, clamped to the first entry if `N >= len`; then `x-real-ip`; then `'unknown'`; never throws (login must not crash — `auth.ts:24-29`). **Two deliberate behaviour deltas:** the five non-`invites` sites gain the `x-real-ip` fallback (consulted only when XFF is absent — strictly additive); `invites` loses its bespoke helper and non-null assertion. Rate-limit keys, thresholds, windows and bucket modules untouched (verified: zero `checkAuthRateLimit` lines in the diff). **CEO DECISION NEEDED:** what sits in front of the app on the VPS and whether it appends or replaces `x-forwarded-for`. Once confirmed the fix is `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=<hops>` in the deploy env — no code change. Original text: **`x-forwarded-for.split(',')[0]` is spoofable, and it gates auth rate limiting** — five call sites derive the client IP from the FIRST entry of `x-forwarded-for`: `api/auth/signup/route.ts:48`, `api/auth/forgot-password/route.ts:20`, `api/admin/bootstrap/route.ts:62`, `lib/auth.ts:30`, and `api/analytics/extension/route.ts:97`. Proxies conventionally APPEND the real client IP, so index `[0]` is whatever the caller supplied unless the edge strips inbound XFF. If it does not, every one of those rate limits is bypassable by rotating a header — which matters far more for **signup, password-reset and login** than for telemetry. **Pre-existing, not introduced by loop 42** (4 of the 5 predate it); surfaced by the `security-reviewer` pass on the new route. Fix: confirm the Railway/Render edge behaviour first, then take the last hop or a configured trusted-proxy count, in ONE shared helper rather than five copies. | fix | security | 4 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 3 | **9** | 2026-09-24 L42 | **centralized (loop 44) — awaiting proxy confirmation to set `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`** |
| 226 | **Extension install counts are inflatable, and the install↔account link may be undisclosed** — two non-blocking findings from the loop-42 security review. (a) The ingest route is unauthenticated by necessity (installs fire before sign-in), so anyone can POST arbitrary `installId`s and inflate install/DAU counts; blast radius is metric integrity, not data exposure. Cheapest mitigation without auth: a per-IP daily cap on DISTINCT new installIds, separate from the request-rate bucket — or state on the dashboard that the number is directionally approximate. (b) `extension_signin_linked` stores `{userId, installId}`, which retroactively de-anonymises earlier `extension_installed` / `extension_session_active` rows sharing that installId. That is ordinary first-party telemetry, not third-party sharing, but the privacy policy should be checked to confirm it discloses install↔account linkage. | improvement | security / privacy | 2 | 4 | 2 | 4 | 2 | 2 | **8** | 2026-09-24 L42 | open |

| 125 | **PATHE-P09 Step-Pair Co-Occurrence Matrix pre-pass + decision confidence calibration** — backend distinctive move NOT in CEO prompt. Build `M[a][b]` = number of runs containing BOTH normalized step labels `a` AND `b` (order-insensitive). O(N × M²) pre-pass where M ≤ 50 distinct labels per workflow. Reveals: (1) **Required co-occurrences** (`M[a][b] / total ≥ 0.90`): identifies suspicious "missing-step" exception paths BEFORE trie construction; (2) **Exclusive steps** (`M[a][b] / total ≤ 0.10` + both ≥40% individual frequency): pre-classifies decision-point candidates at confidence × 1.2 (cap 1.0). **Dramatically reduces decision-detection false positives**. | ~~227~~ | ~~**Duplicate standard deviation + computed-but-unexposed percentiles**~~ — **CLOSED loop 52, all three parts.** (1) `stdDevMs` removed from `TimestudyResult.totalDuration`; `variance.durationVariance.stdDevMs` is now the single source, chosen because it is the one every production surface already reads (report page, scorecard, verdict, evidence, and the column shipped at loop 50). The deleted copy had **zero production readers** — its only two references were its own test assertions, which were moved to the surviving source rather than dropped. (2) `cycle_time_p90_ms`, `cycle_time_min_ms`, `cycle_time_max_ms` now `available`, wired the same five layers; p90 gates at N≥5, min/max at N≥2 because an extreme is meaningful from two runs whereas a percentile is not. (3) `cycle_time_p95_ms` stays `pending` — honestly, since the engine computes p90 — with a user-facing description that says so and points at P90. Validation: web-app **3140 → 3153**, intelligence-engine 238 unchanged, workspace **4940**, typecheck 0. Original text: **The same standard deviation is computed twice, and two computed percentiles reach no column** — found at loop 51 while auditing whether "the value already existed and the consumer discarded it" is a systematic class. Three verified facts. (1) **Duplicate computation:** `timestudyAnalyzer.ts:110` (`totalDuration.stdDevMs`) and `varianceAnalyzer.ts:54` (`durationVariance.stdDevMs`) both call `stdDev(durations)` over an **identically-built** array (`bundles.map(b => b.processRun.durationMs).filter(...)` — byte-identical in both files). They cannot disagree today, same function same input, but two sources of truth for one statistic is exactly how the MDR-P05 shadow-function divergence arose, and loop 50 wired a column to one of them without knowing the other existed. (2) **Computed but unexposed:** `timestudy.totalDuration` carries `p90Ms`, `minMs` and `maxMs`, all stored in `intelligenceJson`; no registry column surfaces any of them. (3) **Pending but uncomputed:** the registry lists `cycle_time_p95_ms` as `pending-path-c-r1`, and the engine computes **p90**, not p95 — so the registry asks for a percentile nobody calculates while ignoring one already in the payload. Same shape as `ai_opportunity_score` being called "entirely missing" when it was already computed. Fix: pick ONE stdDev source and delete the other, add p90/min/max columns, and re-classify or drop `cycle_time_p95_ms`. Related but larger: MR-033 §8.1 measured that `IntelligenceJsonSchema` declares 2 of the 18 keys the payload carries. | fix | analytics / single-source-of-truth | 4 | 5 | 3 | 5 | 2 | 2 | **13** | 2026-09-27 L51 | **done (loop 52)** |
| 110 | **⚠ SCORE CELL IS NOT MACHINE-READABLE (MR-034, loop 55).** A stray `|` in this row's prose splits its cells, so a positional parser misreads the score — #110 reads as **2** against its true 13. Not corruption: the cell count is identical at loops 50, 51 and HEAD. But any automated ranking silently mis-sorts this row, and eight rows share the defect (#45 #223 #157 #152 #127 #126 #121 #110), several in the score band where selection happens. Escape the literal `|` as `\|` when next editing this row. Original text: **DETERMINISM HALF CLOSED at loop 51 — schema-versioning half remains open.** The third and last wall-clock leak is gone: `interpretWorkflow` now takes a mandatory `referenceNowMs` and derives `computedAt` from it, with the boundary created at its single call site (`api/upload/route.ts:159`). **`process-engine` now contains zero wall-clock reads** — verified by grep and pinned by a drift-guard test that scans every source file in the package and fails if `new Date()` or `Date.now()` returns. Loops 43 + 45 + 51 close the family. Mandatory rather than defaulted: one call site, and a default would quietly re-admit the non-determinism the parameter exists to remove. **Still open, and deliberately not bundled** (different logical outcome): the `sopSchemaVersion` closed-union and the pure `migrateSOP` adapter — both verified at 0 hits repo-wide, so that is design work, not a finish. Re-score the remainder before selecting. Original text: **PARTIALLY DONE 2026-09-24 (loop 43): 2 of 3 determinism leaks closed.** `markdownRenderer.ts:313` and `:596` no longer fall back to `new Date().toISOString()`; `renderMetadataStrip` now takes `generatedAt?` and **omits the date segment when it is unknown** rather than inventing one. **Deliberate divergence from this row's prescription:** the row says *make `generatedAt` mandatory — caller must supply*. I did not, because `OperatorSOP.generatedAt` (`templateTypes.ts:245`) and `DecisionSOP.generatedAt` (`:351`) are **already declared optional**, and the honest rendering of an unknown date is omission, not a required-but-fabricated value. **Why this was reachable and not merely a type hole:** `api/workflows/[id]/export-markdown/route.ts:60` does `JSON.parse(artifact.contentJson)` straight into `renderSOPMarkdown` — an untyped boundary — three lines beneath its own comment promising *"repeated exports of the same workflow are byte-identical"*. A stored artifact lacking `generatedAt` printed **today's** date as its evidence date, and a **different** one on every export, breaking the guarantee that comment claims. **Still open:** the third leak, `workflowInterpreter.ts:161` (`computedAt: new Date().toISOString()`), plus the `sopSchemaVersion` closed-union and the `migrateSOP` adapter — none of which this loop touched. ## Candidate Details

### 1. Replace duplicated background logic with workspace package imports
- Type: improvement
- Area: extension architecture
- Problem: the extension background layer duplicates normalization, segmentation, and policy logic instead of importing from workspace packages.
- Evidence: listed as the top active Phase 1 priority and explicitly tracked technical debt in the current engineering brief.
- Expected benefit: stronger determinism, less divergence risk, cleaner package boundaries, easier maintenance.
- Dependencies: verify package interfaces are stable; confirm extension build wiring.
- Impact (1-5): 5
- Strategic alignment (1-5): 5
- Learning value (1-5): 4
- Confidence (1-5): 5
- Effort (1-5): 3
- Risk (1-5): 2
- Priority score: 14
- Recommended next action: select for the next bounded loop unless a blocking reliability issue supersedes it.
- Notes: this is the best current blend of impact, feasibility, and system simplification.

### 2. Persist full session event stream for service worker restart recovery
- Type: fix
- Area: session durability
- Problem: session data is not fully persisted to `chrome.storage.local`; only meta is stored, which weakens recovery after service worker restart.
- Evidence: explicitly listed in known issues and active priorities.
- Expected benefit: stronger resilience, less data loss risk, more trustworthy capture pipeline.
- Dependencies: storage strategy, serialization boundaries, recovery-state validation.
- Impact (1-5): 5
- Strategic alignment (1-5): 5
- Learning value (1-5): 4
- Confidence (1-5): 4
- Effort (1-5): 4
- Risk (1-5): 3
- Priority score: 11
- Recommended next action: keep at the top of the queue; likely follows the package deduplication work.
- Notes: mission-critical for trust and recovery.

### 3. Integrate `@ledgerium/policy-engine` into `content/capture.ts`
- Type: fix
- Area: capture pipeline
- Problem: `content/capture.ts` still uses a local sensitivity pattern instead of the shared policy engine.
- Evidence: explicitly listed in known issues.
- Expected benefit: consistent policy application, less duplication, cleaner trust model.
- Dependencies: import path validation and content-script compatibility.
- Impact (1-5): 4
- Strategic alignment (1-5): 5
- Learning value (1-5): 3
- Confidence (1-5): 5
- Effort (1-5): 2
- Risk (1-5): 2
- Priority score: 13
- Recommended next action: strong low-risk candidate if the next loop favors a smaller change.
- Notes: likely fast win.

### 4. Add Playwright E2E tests for recording lifecycle

- Risk (1-5): 2

### 5. Add structured error logging with session context

## Selection Rules

See `CLAUDE.md § Selection Policy` for the authoritative policy.

**Portfolio overrides** (any overrides top-score):
1. Release-blocker minimum cadence (1-in-5)
2. Area saturation rule (no 3-in-a-row same Area)
3. Follow-up burn-down (1-in-5 targets a prior follow-up)
4. Pool-size density ceiling (pool > 8 → forced burn-down) — MR-002 Change C
5. Ceiling-rule cool-off (after 3 consecutive ceiling-forced burn-downs, next iter may ignore clause 4 once, single-use) — MR-003 Change B

**Within those constraints, prefer:**
1. the highest final score
2. lower-risk items among close scores
3. items that improve determinism, traceability, recovery, and validation
4. reversible changes
5. **exactly one item per iteration**

The iteration log's "Candidate Selection" block MUST state which rule drove the selection: `top-score`, `blocker-cadence`, `saturation-rule`, `burn-down`, `ceiling-cool-off`, or `directed`.
